1Factorisations Of Cunningham/Mersenne Numbers C-(2,n) = M(n) = 2^n - 1
   2
   3Canonical Representation Of Factorisations
   4
   51. Factorisations are given from smallest to largest factor, with a period '.'
   6   separating factors.
   72. Multiplicity of factors is indicated with the carat '^' in ascii tradition
   8   for the text version of the tables but not the HTML version.
   93. The backslash '\' is used to indicate continuation of a line, the decimal
  10   digit representation of the number does not stop but continues on the next
  11   line as if no break had occured. This can sometimes make for bad breaks, but
  12   such is life.
  13
  14Notation Used In The Tables
  15
  161. #Fac is the number of (non distinct) prime factors in an expression.
  172. A plussed #Fac means that more prime factors exist but they are unknown due
  18   to an unfactored composite in the expression.
  19
